# Upstream drift baseline (the live-question artifact)

Generated 2026-11-14 by `scripts/upstream-drift.mjs` (unit DRIFT) — the
behavioral census's manual drift mapping, automated. This file is the
baseline demonstration of what a real re-pin report looks like: it answers
the live question "what would a re-pin of the vendored `gitea/` submodule
actually touch in OUR landed pages and tests?" for the window of ten
upstream commits BEFORE our current pin.

- Window: `fc49368845~10` → `fc49368845` (fc49368845 is the pin the census
  derives all citations against; the submodule HEAD matches it).
- Cite side: `to` — every cite in our tree is valid at the TO endpoint, so
  the "moved" verdicts report where the cited content sat BEFORE the window.
- Regenerate any window with:
  `node scripts/upstream-drift.mjs --from <sha> --to <sha> [--html out.html]`
  or `node scripts/upstream-drift.mjs --current` (working submodule vs the
  recorded gitlink).

How to read the three severity buckets: **(a) CITED-LINE MOVED** — the cite
still points at intact upstream text but the line number shifted; refresh the
cite and re-check the derivation. **(b) CITED REGION CHANGED** — the cited
lines themselves sit inside a changed hunk; the ported behavior may diverge
and must be re-derived. **(c) FILE ADDED/REMOVED** — new or removed upstream
surface (removed files make our cites dead). Informational sections cover
changed upstream surface no cite covers, ambiguous cites whose candidates
changed (unattributable drift), tests likely in scope (name-stem match), and
census-board hints.

Validation of the tool at generation time (honest, three legs):

1. `--selftest`: 39/39 checks — hunk parser (omitted/zero counts), the
   classification math on both cite sides (incl. the zero-count hunk-boundary
   rule, asserted against a real temp-git fixture), the full citation grammar
   (slash/comma/range specs, shorthand binding, `https://`/`12:30` guards),
   the path resolver (suffix match + line-count narrowing + ambiguity), and
   an end-to-end `analyze()` over the temp-git fixture.
2. Hand-verified three sampled files from a real window (HEAD~5..HEAD) by
   reading `git diff -U0` and comparing content: `milestone_list.go` cited
   78-92 shifted -6 and old lines 72-86 are byte-identical to new 78-92;
   `issue_list.go` cited 661 shifted -1 and old 660 == new 661;
   `milestone.go` cite `:35` correctly untouched (three 1:1 hunks, zero net
   shift) while cite `139-200` correctly lands in bucket (b) (overlaps
   `@@ -161 +161 @@`).
3. This baseline is leg 3 — a real window against the live index.

The tool's full stdout for the window follows, verbatim.

# Upstream drift: gitea/ c45af635e4..fc49368845

**Cite side: to** — neither endpoint is the working HEAD (fc49368845); defaulting to the newer endpoint — treat move distances as approximate.

## Summary

- Window: `fc49368845` → `fc49368845` (feat: Allow automatic project actions on events (#30205) → feat: Improve Gitea-compatible forge migrations, fix many migration bugs)
- Upstream files touched: **100** (54 modified, 44 added, 2 deleted, 0 type-changed)
- Citation index: **27820** cite sites in **1018** of our files (24426 explicit, 2364 shorthand, 1030 unbound shorthand, 2272 unresolved, 5911 ambiguous)

- (b) CITED REGION CHANGED: **71** cite sites across 42 our files
- (c) FILE ADDED/REMOVED: **46** upstream files (44 added / 2 removed)
- Changed upstream surface with NO citation coverage: **70** files
- Our files with affected cites: **291**; tests likely in scope (stem match): **255**
- Ambiguous cites whose candidates changed (unattributable drift, informational): **58**
